I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have transparent boxes containing opaque solid spheres
2. We're analyzing cross-sections of these spheres at different z-levels (horizontal planes)
3. At each z-level, the cross-section forms circles (discs) in the xy-plane
4. Connected figures are unions of discs that intersect or touch each other
5. We need to find when the number of connected components changes (increases or decreases)
6. We output the transition pattern as a binary string where 1 = increment, 0 = decrement

## Key Insights

1. For a sphere at (Xi, Yi, Zi) with radius Ri, at height z, the cross-section is a circle with:
   - Center: (Xi, Yi)
   - Radius: √(Ri² - (z - Zi)²) when |z - Zi| ≤ Ri
   - No cross-section when |z - Zi| > Ri

2. The number of connected components changes only at critical z-values where:
   - A sphere starts/exits the cross-section (z = Zi ± Ri)
   - Two circles become tangent or intersect (they connect or disconnect)

3. We need to find all critical z-values and evaluate the number of connected components at each interval.

## Algorithm Steps

1. **Find all critical z-values**: 
   - For each sphere, add Zi - Ri and Zi + Ri
   - For each pair of spheres, find intersection points between their cross-sections

2. **Sort critical points** and remove duplicates

3. **For each interval between critical points**:
   - Determine which spheres contribute (their z-range)
   - Create circles from active spheres
   - Find connected components using Union-Find (Disjoint Set Union)

4. **Track transitions**: When going from one interval to the next, check if the number of components increases or decreases

5. **Build the binary string**: 1 for increase, 0 for decrease

## Implementation Details

1. Check if two circles intersect: 
   - Two circles with centers (x1,y1), (x2,y2) and radii r1, r2 intersect if distance between centers ≤ r1 + r2

2. Use Union-Find to group intersecting circles

```python
import math
from collections import defaultdict

def distance(point1, point2):
    return math.sqrt((point1[0] - point2[0])**2 + (point1[1] - point2[1])**2)

def circles_intersect(center1, radius1, center2, radius2):
    d = distance(center1, center2)
    return d <= radius1 + radius2 and d >= abs(radius1 - radius2)

def find_connected_components(circles):
    if not circles:
        return 0
    
    n = len(circles)
    parent = list(range(n))
    
    def find(x):
        if parent[x] != x:
            parent[x] = find(parent[x])
        return parent[x]
    
    def union(x, y):
        px, py = find(x), find(y)
        if px != py:
            parent[px] = py
    
    # Check all pairs of circles
    for i in range(n):
        for j in range(i + 1, n):
            if circles_intersect(circles[i][0:2], circles[i][2], circles[j][0:2], circles[j][2]):
                union(i, j)
    
    # Count distinct components
    components = len(set(find(i) for i in range(n)))
    return components

def solve():
    while True:
        n = int(input().strip())
        if n == 0:
            break
            
        spheres = []
        for _ in range(n):
            x, y, z, r = map(int, input().split())
            spheres.append((x, y, z, r))
        
        # Find all critical z values
        critical_z = set()
        
        # Add z ± r for each sphere
        for x, y, z, r in spheres:
            critical_z.add(z - r)
            critical_z.add(z + r)
        
        # Add intersection points between spheres
        for i in range(n):
            for j in range(i + 1, n):
                x1, y1, z1, r1 = spheres[i]
                x2, y2, z2, r2 = spheres[j]
                
                # Check if they intersect in 3D space
                # Two spheres intersect in a circle if |z1-z2| <= r1+r2 and |z1-z2| >= |r1-r2|
                z_diff = abs(z1 - z2)
                if z_diff <= r1 + r2 and z_diff >= abs(r1 - r2):
                    # Find z where the intersection occurs
                    # The intersection circle exists at certain z values
                    # The distance between centers in xy-plane is d_xy
                    d_xy = math.sqrt((x1 - x2)**2 + (y1 - y2)**2)
                    
                    # The intersection occurs in a plane at z such that:
                    # (z - z1)^2 + (z - z2)^2 <= r1^2 + r2^2 - d_xy^2
                    # Actually, for each z between z1-r1 and z2-r2, there might be intersection
                    
                    # The intersection exists for z where |z - z1| <= r1 and |z - z2| <= r2,
                    # and the circles intersect in the xy-plane
                    min_z = max(z1 - r1, z2 - r2)
                    max_z = min(z1 + r1, z2 + r2)
                    
                    if min_z < max_z:
                        # Add the boundaries where the intersection starts/stops
                        critical_z.add(min_z)
                        critical_z.add(max_z)
        
        # Convert to sorted list
        critical_z = sorted(critical_z)
        
        # If no critical points, there's no transition
        if len(critical_z) <= 1:
            print(0)
            print("")
            continue
            
        # Find transitions
        transitions = []
        
        # We need to evaluate at each interval between critical points
        for i in range(len(critical_z) - 1):
            z_start = critical_z[i]
            z_end = critical_z[i + 1]
            
            # Check if this interval has a transition (from previous to current)
            # First, get the state at the previous interval
            if i == 0:
                prev_components = 0
            else:
                # We need to simulate the state at z_start
                # But we need to be careful - we're not evaluating at the exact point
                # Let's evaluate at the left boundary of this interval
                prev_components = get_components_at_z(spheres, critical_z[i])
            
            # Now get current state (at the right boundary)
            current_components = get_components_at_z(spheres, critical_z[i + 1])
            
            # But we need to be careful - the transition happens at critical points
            # The state changes exactly at the critical points
            # So we compare components at consecutive critical points
            
            # Actually, let's re-think: we evaluate the state at each critical point
            # The transition happens when we move from one critical point to the next
            
            # We'll recalculate all states at critical points
            pass
        
        # Let's simplify: evaluate components at each critical point
        component_counts = []
        
        for z in critical_z:
            # Find which spheres are present at this z
            circles = []
            for x, y, z_s, r in spheres:
                if abs(z - z_s) <= r:
                    radius = math.sqrt(r * r - (z - z_s) * (z - z_s))
                    circles.append(((x, y), radius))
            
            # Find connected components
            count = find_connected_components(circles)
            component_counts.append(count)
        
        # Now find transitions between consecutive states
        transitions = []
        for i in range(1, len(component_counts)):
            if component_counts[i] > component_counts[i-1]:
                transitions.append('1')
            else:
                transitions.append('0')
        
        # Output
        print(len(transitions))
        if transitions:
            print(''.join(transitions))
        else:
            print("")

def get_components_at_z(spheres, z):
    circles = []
    for x, y, z_center, r in spheres:
        if abs(z - z_center) <= r:
            radius = math.sqrt(r * r - (z - z_center) * (z - z_center))
            circles.append(((x, y), radius))
    
    if not circles:
        return 0
        
    n = len(circles)
    parent = list(range(n))
    
    def find(x):
        if parent[x] != x:
            parent[x] = find(parent[x])
        return parent[x]
    
    def union(x, y):
        px, py = find(x), find(y)
        if px != py:
            parent[px] = py
    
    for i in range(n):
        for j in range(i + 1, n):
            d = distance(circles[i][0], circles[j][0])
            if d <= circles[i][1] + circles[j][1]:
                union(i, j)
    
    return len(set(find(i) for i in range(n)))
```

```python
import math

def distance(p1, p2):
    return math.sqrt((p1[0] - p2[0])**2 + (p1[1] - p2[1])**2)

def circles_intersect(c1, c2):
    # c1 and c2 are (center, radius) tuples
    d = distance(c1[0], c2[0])
    r1, r2 = c1[1], c2[1]
    return d <= r1 + r2 and d >= abs(r1 - r2)

        return 0

    parent = list(range(n))

        return parent[x]

        if px != py:
            parent[px] = py

            if circles_intersect(circles[i], circles[j]):
                union(i, j)
    
    # Count distinct components
    components = len(set(find(i) for i in range(n)))
    return components

                # They intersect if |z1 - z2| <= r1 + r2 and |z1 - z2| >= |r1 - r2|
                z_diff = abs(z1 - z2)
                if z_diff <= r1 + r2 and z_diff >= abs(r1 - r2):
                    # Find the z ranges where intersection occurs
                    # For a given z, the circles exist if |z - z1| <= r1 and |z - z2| <= r2
                    min_z = max(z1 - r1, z2 - r2)
                    max_z = min(z1 + r1, z2 + r2)
                    
                    if min_z < max_z:
                        # The intersection z-range is [min_z, max_z]

                transitions.append('0')

            print(''.join(transitions))
        else:
            print("")
```

    r1, r2 = c1[1], c2[1]
